# Syndicate — Work Lanes

> Lanes are exclusive by file path. Two lanes may not claim the same file.
> Max concurrent OPEN lanes: 3 (see `state.md`).
> Managed by `/lane`. Do not hand-edit while a session is running.

> **History lives in `lanes_history.md`.** This file is read at the start of
> every session, so it carries each lane's CURRENT state plus one prior block --
> **plus any block that declares file claims**, which `lane-guard` reads from
> here and nowhere else. 36 superseded blocks (2,667 lines) were moved out
> verbatim on 2026-08-18. Nothing was summarised or deleted: if a lane's earlier
> reasoning matters, it is there under the same slug.

markers for running sessions did NOT match any roster id, so the mapping proves
death, never life — do not invert it.

### mlb-roster-substrate-probe — OPEN — opened 2026-09-10 — session 2edf8b82-9f8a-4d32-bf26-ca43ecd1ea5a
- Goal: answer ONE question that cannot be answered from outside refresh-worker — **does `daily_pitcher_props/snapshots/<date>/roster_objs/roster_obj_*.json` resolve on that service, and how many of its dates are `>= 2026-07-20`** (clear of the HRR contamination boundary `#624` step 2 records). A boot-time PROBE that reports; it changes no model and writes no artifact.
- WHY IT IS A PROBE AND NOT THE RE-FIT: `#624` step 3's re-fit reads that path, the local checkout has only 13 dates and **every one is at or before the HRR boundary**, and production's export API cannot see the tree at all. Shipping a re-fit runner on an unverified path is the deployed-inert shape `#625`(3) exists to catch — and this session already hit that shape once tonight (the NFL join stamped 0 rows against real data while 13 unit tests passed).
- IT IS A CLASS, NOT ONE SCRIPT: **11 scripts read that path**, including `sim_input_checklist.py`, which `CLAUDE.md` names as the GATING input checklist every model engine must pass.
- Hypothesis: refresh-worker HAS the tree (it writes roster artifacts — `--write-roster-artifacts` defaults `on` and production does not override it) but possibly under `data/daily/snapshots/` rather than `daily_pitcher_props/snapshots/`, which is a DIFFERENT tree and the one the re-fit cannot reach via `SYNDICATE_MLB_DATA_ROOT` (the suffix is hard-coded).
- Falsification test: if the probe prints a count for BOTH trees and they disagree with this hypothesis, the hypothesis is wrong and the re-fit's path handling — not the data — is the defect.
- Verification: a `[refresh_worker] MLB_ROSTER_SUBSTRATE` line on refresh-worker after deploy, printed UNCONDITIONALLY including when both trees are absent, so an absent tree and a failed probe are distinguishable.
- Cost: one directory scan at BOOT ONLY. Not per-tick — `learnings.md`/`#241`: worker periodic work is never free.
- Files: NONE claimed. The only write is ADDITIVE (one boot-probe function plus one call line) into a file OPEN lane `ncaaf-live-resim-wire` holds — **declared in that lane's block rather than claimed here**, because two OPEN holders on one path is what `check_lane_invariants` refuses.

There is a test for precisely that.
- **VERIFICATION FOR ITEM 2, 6 tests green in `tests/test_daily_update_secret_redaction.py`, written so they can fail.** `off != on` (the unprotected hashtable DOES serialise the sentinel, so the rest test the fix and not the fixture); source hashtable unmutated; ordinary values survive; **a STRUCTURAL test that every `environmentOverrides` assignment in the script is wrapped** — the one that catches a NEW leak site, since the behavioural tests only prove the function works, not that it is called; and the committed reports stay scrubbed, matched on SHAPE (32-hex under an `environmentOverrides` key) rather than the one known value. **Scrub was targeted at the value spans, not a reserialisation** — diffs are 8/8, 2/2, 1/1, 7/7 lines instead of four reformatted files; each file re-parsed as JSON before writing; the 4,700-line script parses clean at 0 AST errors. **Swept afterwards: no credential-named field in any tracked `reports/*.json` holds a value other than `<redacted>`.** The ONLY credential literals left in the repo are the three `ODDS_API_KEY` entries in `render.yaml`, which parked commit `b3ebd0cb` removes and which are held pending the push decision. **Git history deliberately untouched** — the exposed key is revoked `[user, 2026-09-09]`, so this is cleanup, and rewriting public history is its own decision.

    - **Where.** `polymarket_us_submitter.build`, after `order_body` validates (the same place as step 1). No other file changes behaviour. `execute_portfolio`, `portfolio_commit` and `check_order` are untouched.
    - **Book.** One signed book read. If the ask for our side is unreadable (read failure, empty side), it REFUSES `ask_unreadable`. Unknown must not default permissive; this reverses step 1's never-block rule on purpose, and only when the switch is on.
    - **EV at the ask, net of fees.**
      - `p_model = planned_p * (1 + ev_pct/100)`, the same fair step 1 logs.
      - `cost = ask + fee_per_contract`, with `fee_per_contract = POLYMARKET_ASSUMED_WORST_CASE_RATE` (0.02, `venue_fees.py:465`). That is a bound above the measured 0.015 at mid-price and the 0.0107 measured at 0.235, stated as a bound.
      - `ev_net_pct = (p_model / cost - 1) * 100`.
      - It refuses `ask_ev_below_min` when `ev_net_pct < resolve_settings().min_ev_pct` (the plan's own minimum, default 2.0).
    - **Kelly at the ask.**
      - `k(c) = (p_model - c) / (1 - c)`.
      - `stake_at_ask = planned_stake * min(1, k(cost) / k(planned_p))`, so it only ever shrinks. `check_order` already charged the planned stake, so no cap can be exceeded.
    - **Price and size.**
      - The price sent is the ask, snapped up to the tick.
      - `quantity = min(floor(stake_at_ask / ask), floor(ask_qty))` to the market's increment.
      - Below one increment it refuses `ask_size_below_minimum`.
    - **Log.** One `POLYMARKET_PRICED_AT_ASK` line per build: planned price and stake, ask and size, fee bound, `ev_net_pct`, Kelly ratio, the stake and quantity sent, and the decision.
    - **Falsification.**

      - (b) With it on, a 20%-planned build whose ask sits 34 ticks above the quote (the measured `aec-nfl-car-atl` shape, planned 17.7, `ev_at_ask` 1.39) must REFUSE.
      - (c) A small-edge build at the quote must place at the ask with its stake at or below plan.
      - (d) A thin ask must cap quantity at `ask_qty`.
      - (e) A read failure must refuse.
      - Any of these failing means step 2 is not what it claims.

From 20:10Z the fingerprint triggers land every ~18-19 min, i.e. a new sim launches as soon as the last finishes — the sim runs back to back and the board waits. One 55-min gap (20:53-21:48Z) left today's board 101 min stale. **Today NOTHING logs which game or which input changed**: `changed_game_pks` is computed at `live_refresh_loop.py:2030` and never printed, and `_record_mlb_sim_check` stores only the combined per-game hash.

NOT touched: the tip-off-window, cold-start, join-mismatch and board-missing branches, and `render.yaml` (it pins `SYNDICATE_MLB_SIM_CHECK_INTERVAL_SECONDS=600`; this fix adds a NEW knob with a code default precisely so no render.yaml push and no blueprint sync is needed).
- Hypotheses, to be told apart by evidence, not argument: (H1) IN-PLAY LINES — `_mlb_sim_odds_fingerprint_slice` drops prices but keeps line values exactly, so a started game's live total moving as runs score re-fires a PREGAME sim that cannot use it. Weakened already: 5 triggers fired 13:35-15:21Z, before the first tip-off window (16:35Z). (H2) PREGAME CHURN ACROSS A 15-GAME SLATE — genuine line moves and lineup/injury postings across 15 games, each legitimate, jointly firing every tick. (H3) A NON-SEMANTIC FLIP in one input (injuries or posted-lineup payloads reordered/restamped) moving the hash with no content change, the same class as the row-ordering bug #48 fixed for odds. (H4) SCOPING DOES NOT SAVE TIME — re-sims are scoped to changed games, yet each takes ~17 min; if one game costs as much as the slate, the fixed cost is the waste, whatever the trigger.
- **H4 CONFIRMED 2026-09-17 23:4xZ, before any code:** a re-sim's duration does not scale with its scope — `--only-game-pks` read from the processes' own command lines: **1 game 14.9 min, 2 games 17.4, 3 games 16.6, 4 games 16.9, 5 games 15.8**, 9 games 20.1. The fixed cost per run is the waste, so every trigger, legitimate or not, buys a ~15-min lockout of the board. Mechanism of the back-to-back chain: the check interval is 600 s (`render.yaml`) and a run takes ~15 min, so the first check after a run always finds SOME change across 15 games. Safe to throttle: `_mlb_daily_sim_decision` returns `tip_off_window` BEFORE the interval check, a running sim short-circuits every check without recording fingerprints (so changes accumulate rather than being absorbed), and prices are joined at read time so a price move needs no re-sim.
- **USER DECISION 2026-09-17 ("Code debounce"):** a minimum gap between FINGERPRINT-triggered launches (new knob, code default 3600 s), leaving tip-off-window, cold-start, join-mismatch, board-missing and props-regen immediate; ships with a normal refresh-worker deploy. Accepted cost: a mid-day lineup or line change waits up to the gap for its re-sim unless it enters the tip-off window.

- Goal: cut refresh-worker's daily prediction-reconciliation autorun, which runs INLINE in the main loop and blocks every book-grid tick while it runs, from ~23 min to <= 5 min, by walking the persistent disk once per date instead of twelve times, with every date's reconciliation result unchanged. Verified by a per-date timing line on the first autorun after deploy.
- Why: USER 2026-09-18 ~19:05 CDT chose "Trace soccer live rebuilds", out of lane `board-build-stage-slowdown`.
  - MEASURED (refresh-worker Render logs, 2026-09-18): the 31-54 min soccer book-grid tick gaps in 18:00-21:10Z were RESTARTS, not live play. There were 12 `WORKER_SHUTDOWN signal=SIGTERM` between 14:40Z and 00:44Z. With no restart (22:20Z-00:57Z, games live) ticks came every 3-15 min.
  - Reconciliation came due at 17:45:37Z. It then logged `RECONCILIATION_AUTORUN_RUNNING` on five boots, with `last_epoch_age_sec` 86454 → 89378 → 91372 → 93238 → 95608 → 98348: never stamped, because each deploy's SIGTERM killed it mid-run. Its one clean run was 21:03:51Z → ~21:27:13Z, **~23.4 min** (stamp age 2083 s at 22:01:56Z).
  - Cost hypothesis, NOT YET SPLIT by any timing line:
    - `_candidate_result_paths` makes six `root.rglob` walks of `/opt/render/project/data`, and it is called twice per date. `DISK_INVENTORY_SUMMARY` 21:03:11Z counts 48,816 dirs and 125,197 files there.
    - 12 walks × 14 dates is 168 walks, about 8 s each.
    - Secondary: `_row_keys` is recomputed per prediction × row (11,201 closing rows on 09-18). A debug JSON of every result row is built per unmatched prediction for a `logger.info` that Render never collects.
- Files: `syndicate/features/prediction_reconciliation.py` (`_candidate_result_paths`, its row loader, `_match_result_row`, `reconcile_prediction_results_for_date` only), `tests/test_reconciliation_disk_walks.py` (NEW). Checked 2026-09-19 ~01:40Z on the main branch at `3f1a0fb6`: no OPEN lane claims `prediction_reconciliation.py`. The refresh-worker main loop script is NOT touched (other lanes claim it), so a cross-date index and persisting the book-grid tick state across restarts are follow-ups, not this lane.
- Design:
  - One `os.scandir` pre-order walk per (root, date) matches all six names. It visits directories in the order `Path.rglob` does on CPython 3.11.9 (what `render.yaml` pins): a directory before its children, children in scandir order, symlinked directories not followed.
  - The path list is computed once per call. Row keys are computed once per date.
  - The unmatched-prediction debug JSON is built only when INFO is enabled.
  - `[prediction_reconciliation] RECONCILE_DATE_TIMING` prints once per date, with the walk, row-read, match and ledger-write seconds.
- Verification (PRE-REGISTERED):
  - (1) Tests:
    - Path lists equal a frozen copy of the old `rglob` implementation's on a nested fixture tree, order included.
    - Each call scans each directory once (six times before).
    - `reconcile_prediction_results_for_date` returns the same payload and ledger results as the old code on the same inputs, including which of two conflicting result files wins.
  - (2) Production, on the first autorun after a refresh-worker deploy carrying it: the `RECONCILE_DATE_TIMING` total_s summed over the run <= 300 s; `RECONCILIATION_AUTORUN_RUNNING` followed by a stamp (the next `GATED` line's age) within 5 min; resolved counts > 0 on dates with results, as before.
  - Rides the next refresh-worker main-tip deploy. It does not need one of its own.
- **STATUS 2026-09-19 ~02:45Z — CODE ON MAIN `9df3f4bc`, NOT DEPLOYED. GOAL: NOT MET** until a refresh-worker deploy carries it and the first autorun after that is read (the next one is due ~21:27Z 09-19 by the stamp, sooner only if a run fails).
  - Verification (1) MET: `tests/test_reconciliation_disk_walks.py` has 10 tests plus 1 skipped (symlinks on Windows). Against HEAD's module, 6 fail (the change tests) and 4 pass (the equivalence tests, which the old code IS the reference for).
  - 104 passed / 1 skipped across the 10 reconciliation suites, plus 3/3 `test_refresh_worker -k reconcil`.
  - Local timing on a 5,550-directory tree: 13.4 s → 0.82 s per date.

Verified offline with a counting fake fetcher (3n calls -> 3 for n matches spread across leagues, ids equal), and on production by re-timing the poller on a comparable in-play slate: FotMob's share of a tick falls from the measured **15%** (lane `soccer-live-loop-cost`, 2026-09-19) to under 6%.
- Files: none held. **Claim released 2026-09-21 on `scripts/poll_soccer_live_state.py`, `tests/test_soccer_live_fotmob_fixture_cache.py` and `tests/test_poll_soccer_live_state.py`: this lane's code is landed (`858a1763`) and it owes only a production timing, while lane `soccer-live-goal-window-from-paths` (same session) had to edit the poller and repoint that test's fixture at the new symbol.**
- Hypothesis: `resolve_fotmob_match_id` fetches `matches_for_date` for THREE dates (offset 0, -1, +1) on every call and caches nothing, and `poll_league` calls it once per in-play match. Every match on the same date asks for the SAME three payloads, so a 12-match tick makes 36 identical-in-effect fixture fetches plus 12 `matchDetails` fetches. Memoising the fixture list for the life of ONE TICK (not across ticks) removes 33 of those 36 without changing an id, and the live series (`matchDetails`) stays uncached because that is the part that moves.
- Falsification test: if the counting fake shows the call count unchanged, the fetches are not going through the injection point this change threads; if any resolved id or momentum block differs from the uncached run on the same fixtures, the cache is not transparent and the change is wrong.
- Verification: (1) offline call-count and equality tests with a counting fake, across two leagues in one tick; (2) the existing live-state tests still pass; (3) production: the poller re-timed against a live slate shows FotMob under 6% of a tick. (3) needs a deploy and is NOT owed before the code lands.
- Blocked by: none. The deploy can ride along with `c612db03` (lane `soccer-live-serialize-fields-race`) or any later live-odds-worker deploy.
- Note: this is the "cached" half of the live-loop cost lead in `leads.md` (FotMob 15% of a tick). The "concurrent" half is NOT taken here — caching makes FEWER outbound requests, concurrency makes the same number burstier, and the first is the safer lever against a third-party API.

### layer2-line-move-magnitude — OPEN — opened 2026-09-20 — session 105fd5dd-14d4-4ec6-904e-057f574d8ad8
- **GOAL VERDICT (2026-09-21, session 9e340058 continuing 105fd5dd).** Goal, verbatim: "the Layer 2 board's LINE-movement magnitude is derived from the LINE DELTA via a MEASURED per-market probability slope, so the magnitude and `movement_vs_pick` can never contradict each other -- verified on the served board by ZERO rows where `movement_vs_pick` disagrees with the sign of `movement_line_prob_delta_pp`, and by the slope being READ from production rather than assumed." — **GOAL: NOT MET, and the lane's framing was WRONG.** The monotonicity check it named as its first step RAN (`deploys.md` n/a — offline, against the recovered d419cc24 board and the live board) and **explains only 14% of the failure**: of 173 sign conflicts, 24 had a non-monotone within-build curve and **149 (86%) had a CORRECTLY monotone curve** whose conflict came from the other term. The dominant defect is upstream of the magnitude entirely -- see the next bullet.
- **2026-09-21 14:41Z -- THE REAL FIX IS DEPLOYED AND VERIFIED, 4 of 4** (`c1710042` + `c70329c2`, refresh-worker live 14:36:42Z, read against `written_at` 14:40:44Z): `line_moved` **913 -> 0**; rows compared with their OWN line's opening **1,949/2,000**; rows at the cap **317 -> 63**; negative control held (`rows_admitted_by_blend` 119 -> 112). The 51 `earliest_line` rows are all moneyline, routed to a price comparison by the `088f39fe` waiver -- checked, not assumed. **`pipeline/layer2_shortlist.py` RETURNED to `board-build-stage-slowdown`** in this commit, per its release in `aa00b139`; the edit is landed and verified. `deploys.md` 14:30Z / 14:41Z.
- **THE REAL DEFECT IS THE OPENINGS INDEX, AND IT IS LIVE.** `pipeline/layer2_shortlist.py` collapses the openings ledger by the LINE-LESS `movement_join_key`, first write wins. The board publishes several lines of one bet AT ONCE (alt markets and main markets alike), so every line but the first-recorded is paired with a DIFFERENT line's opening and classified `line_moved`. **Measured on the live board (`written_at` 2026-09-21T13:52:19Z): 538 of 912 line-moved rows (59%) have their opening line STILL PUBLISHED in the same build** -- a lower bound, served rows being a subset of the grid. **506 of them are currently SCORED on that comparison.**
- **THOSE ROWS DOMINATE THE LIVE TERM'S MASS.** Their `|movement_component|` median is **0.975** against **0.352** for the rest; **233 sit at the cap**; 9 are in the board's top 50 and 54 in the top 200. Split boost 272 / penalty 234, so it is NOISE, not bias -- consistent with measuring the fair gap between two different lines rather than a market move. The same-bet magnitude (d419cc24) conflicted for the same root reason: the opening it read at `L0` was often not the opening of the bet it was scoring.
- **WHAT THIS DOES TO THE 2026-09-20 VERIFICATION.** "1,063 rows now score a line move" verified that rows RECEIVED a score, not that the moves were REAL. It was never checked whether the opening line had actually gone. That is presence read as correctness, and it is recorded in `learnings.md`.
- **SIDE-MIRRORING BUG, real but minor.** The reverted `build_line_curve` filed both sides under the ROW's `line`, ignoring `_side_line_from_cells`, so a spread's away fair sat under home's number. Only 7 of the 174 conflicts were spreads; the dramatic `away -1.5 +37 pp` example quoted all evening was unrepresentative. Must be fixed in any retry.
- **2026-09-21 -- USER: "do the real fix". BUILT, TESTED, LANDED, AND INERT UNTIL WIRED.** `index_openings` + a same-line lookup in `_movement_from_opening` landed as `c1710042` (`layer2_board.py`, this lane's file). **REPLAYED WITH THE REAL FUNCTIONS against the real 2026-09-21 ledger** (4.75 MB, under the export cap; cut 20 min before the build so it cannot see its own appends), after first confirming the CURRENT logic over that ledger reproduces production's classification **1,988/1,988**: `line_moved` **903 -> 23**, same-line price comparisons **1,083 -> 1,963**, components at the cap **328 -> 50**. Rows move onto the EXISTING calibrated price path -- no new estimator.
- **WHAT IS NOT DONE, AND WHY: the wiring in `pipeline/layer2_shortlist.py`.** That file is held by OPEN lane `board-build-stage-slowdown` (session a1e40980), scoped to the `_profiled_by_date` decorator only. A SCOPED CO-CLAIM was requested by message (queued 2026-09-21 ~14:2xZ) -- for this lane to make the edit itself, not for that lane to make it. **Not taken while unanswered.** The patch is ready and `git apply --check` clean: `scratchpad/wire_openings_by_line.patch`, +30/-14 across three spots (the openings build ~`:909`, `build_layer2_rows` ~`:1602` behind an `inspect.signature` probe, `card_extras` ~`:1967`). **Deploying `c1710042` alone would change NOTHING** -- the builder passes no per-line index -- so no deploy until wired.
- **VERIFICATION, pre-registered for the deploy:** on the served board, `movement.movement_opening_match` is `same_line` on the large majority of keyed rows and `earliest_line` on a small residue (replay: 1,963 / 23); `line_moved` rows fall from ~900 to tens; components at the cap fall from ~330 to ~50; and `rows_admitted_by_blend` stays in band as the negative control. If `earliest_line` dominates, the index is not reaching the builder.
- Goal: the Layer 2 board's LINE-movement magnitude is derived from the LINE DELTA via a MEASURED per-market probability slope, so the magnitude and `movement_vs_pick` can never contradict each other -- verified on the served board by ZERO rows where `movement_vs_pick` disagrees with the sign of `movement_line_prob_delta_pp`, and by the slope being READ from production rather than assumed.
- Files: `syndicate/features/shared/layer2_board.py`, `syndicate/features/shared/opportunity_signals.py`, `tests/test_layer2_line_movement_scoring.py`, `scripts/measure_line_probability_slope.py`, NEW `tests/test_line_probability_slope.py`
- Hypothesis: a line move's honest magnitude can be read from the same bet repriced at its own line. **FALSIFIED as implemented** — not in principle, but because its input (alternate-line fairs) fails a consistency the approach silently assumed.
- **NEXT, and it is the first step, not an optional one:** measure alternate-line fair consistency directly — within ONE build a side's fair must be MONOTONE in its line — and admit only identities that pass. Offline, no deploy; `measure_line_probability_slope.py` already walks that data. Restore `movement_line_sign_conflict` before any retry: it is what turned a silent wrong signal into one deploy's worth of evidence.
- **STANDS:** same-bet coverage 52% (exact+interpolated), and the four-gate slope tool (n, days agree, over/under symmetry, sign test). Only the assumption beneath them failed.

## 2026-08-17 - THE LEDGER IS A RECORD, NOT EVIDENCE (the inverse of the same day's other lesson)

I relayed *"two uncommitted soccer fixes at risk of being lost"* to the
coordinator as an action item. **It came from a lane entry, not from a
measurement.** `git status` was empty and fix #1 was already on main.

**This is the exact inverse of the three errors recorded above it today.** There
I called healthy things BROKEN from a null lookup. Here I called a committed
thing AT RISK from a written claim I never checked. **Same root cause: treating
a statement as a reading.**

`.syndicate/**` records what was true WHEN WRITTEN. This lane was last touched
two days before I quoted it. **Before acting on or forwarding a ledger claim
about the state of the working tree - uncommitted work, missing files, a broken
service - re-measure it.** The cost here was small (a wrong action item, since
retracted). The cost of the reverse - deleting or "rescuing" files on a stale
claim - would not have been.
